     DEED OF NON-COMPETITION
     Deed of Non-competition of Controlling Shareholders
     To safeguard the Group from any potential competition from the Controlling Shareholders (as defined below), each of the
     Controlling Shareholders has entered into the Controlling Shareholders’ Deed of Non-competition (as defined below) in
     favour of the Group with details as set out below.




44   Nanshan Aluminium International Holdings Limited   Annual Report 2025
                                                                                           Report of the Directors

The Controlling Shareholders, namely NAIHL, NAS, Nanshan Aluminium, Yili Electric, Nanshan Group and Nanshan Village
Committee, entered into a deed of non-competition in favour of the Company (for itself and as trustee for the subsidiaries
of the Company) dated 10 March 2025 (the “Controlling Shareholders’ Deed of Non-Competition”), pursuant to which
the Controlling Shareholders have irrevocably and unconditionally undertaken to the Company (the “Non-Competition
Undertakings”) that, subject to certain permitted exceptions, during the term of the Controlling Shareholders’ Deed of
Non-Competition, they shall not, and shall procure their respective close associates and/or the companies controlled by
them (other than members of the Group) not to, directly or indirectly, either on their own account or in conjunction with or
on behalf of any person, firm or company, partnership, joint venture, or other contractual arrangement, whether for profit
or not, carry on, engage in, control (in each case whether as a shareholder, partner, agent, employee or otherwise and
whether for profit, reward or otherwise) any business which is or may be similar to or in competition with any business
including the production and sales of alumina (the “Restricted Business”) currently and from time to time engaged by
the Group in the countries and regions in which any member of the Group currently carries out the business of production
and sales of alumina products. If any member of our Group expands its business to other countries and regions in the
future, such additional countries and regions will automatically be included in the restraint area, and the undertakings and
obligations of the Controlling Shareholders made will be applicable to such additional countries and regions, but Mainland
China is not included in the restraint area at any time (the “Restraint Area”).

Under the Controlling Shareholders’ Deed of Non-Competition, the Controlling Shareholders further undertook that if they
and/or any of their close associates has received, is offered or has identified any business investment or other business
opportunity that competes or may compete, directly or indirectly, with the Restricted Business in the Restraint Area (the
“New Business Opportunity”), they and/or any of their close associates shall (1) promptly (in any event not later than
seven days) give a notice in writing to the Company in respect of such New Business Opportunity, setting out all reasonably
necessary information for the Group to make an informed assessment; and (2) use their best efforts to assist the Company
in acquiring such New Business Opportunity at terms and conditions no less favourable than those available to them and/or
their close associates.

For further details of the Controlling Shareholders’ Deed of Non-Competition, please refer to the section headed
“Relationship with Controlling Shareholders” in the Prospectus. The independent non-executive Directors had reviewed
the status of compliance and the confirmation provided by the Controlling Shareholders as part of the annual review
process. On the basis that: (i) the Company has received the confirmations from its Controlling Shareholders regarding the
Non-Competition Undertakings; (ii) there was no competing business reported by the Controlling Shareholders; and (iii)
there was no particular situation rendering the full compliance of the Non-Competition Undertakings being questionable, the
independent non-executive Directors are of the view that the Non-Competition Undertakings have been complied with and
been enforced by the Company in accordance with the terms.

Deed of Non-competition of Executive Directors and Non-executive Directors
To safeguard the Group from any potential competition from our executive Directors and non-executive Directors, each
of our executive Directors and non-executive Directors has entered into the deed of non-competition dated 10 March
2025 (the “Directors’ Deed of Non-competition”) in favour of our Company (for itself and as trustee for each of our
subsidiaries) to provide certain non-competition undertakings. Ms. Han Yanhong, who joined the Board as an executive
Director on 21 November 2025, also provided similar non-competition undertakings to the Group in the service agreement
entered into between her and the Company.

Our Company will make appropriate disclosure regarding the compliance of the Directors’ Deed of Non-competition in its
subsequent annual reports.

DIRECTORS’ PERMITTED INDEMNITY PROVISION
Under the articles of association of the Company, every Director or other officer of the Company shall be entitled to be
indemnified out of the assets of the Company against all losses or liabilities incurred or sustained by him as a Director
or other officer of the Company in defending any proceedings, whether civil or criminal, in which judgment is given in
his favour, or in which he is acquitted. The Company has arranged appropriate directors’ and officers’ liability insurance
coverage for the Directors and officers of the Group.

     CONTINUING CONNECTED TRANSACTIONS
     Connected Persons
     (i)     Nanshan Aluminium is a company established in the PRC with limited liability and is listed on the Shanghai Stock
             Exchange (stock code: 600219). Nanshan Aluminium Group is an integrated aluminium group, and principally engaged
             in the provision of cold-rolled products, hot-rolled products, aluminium profiles, alloy ingots and aluminium foils. It is
             ultimately owned as to approximately 59.96% of the issued share capital of the Company as at 31 December 2025
             through various intermediate holding companies. Accordingly, Nanshan Aluminium is a Controlling Shareholder of the
             Company and therefore a connected person of the Company under the Listing Rules.

     (ii)    Press Metal is a company listed on the Main Market of Bursa Malaysia Securities Berhad (stock code: 8869) and is
             principally engaged in the manufacturing and trading of primary, value-added and extruded aluminium products. Press
             Metal indirectly holds approximately 21.71% of the issued share capital of the Company as at 31 December 2025
             through its wholly-owned subsidiary, PMIRHK. Accordingly, Press Metal is a substantial shareholder of the Company
             and therefore a connected person of the Company under the Listing Rules.

     (iii)   Mr. George Santos is a non-executive Director of the Company. As such, he is a connected person of the Company
             under the Listing Rules.

     (iv)    Mr. Santony is the father of Mr. George Santos. Therefore, Mr. Santony is an associate of Mr. George Santos and a
             connected person of the Company under the Listing Rules.

     (v)     MKU is a company incorporated in Indonesia and is primarily engaged in mining and excavation of bauxite, aluminium
             and other minerals, and trading in metals and metal ores. MKU, being owned as to 99% by Mr. Santony and 1% by
             Mr. George Santos, is an associate of Mr. George Santos, and therefore a connected person of the Company under
             the Listing Rules.

Continuing Connected Transactions Exempt from Independent Shareholders’ Approval Requirement
1.     Technical Support Framework Agreement
       Principal terms
       On 10 March 2025, the Company (for itself and as trustee for each member of the Group) entered into a technical
       support framework agreement with Nanshan Aluminium (for itself and as trustee for each member of Nanshan
       Aluminium Group, excluding the Group) (the “Technical Support Framework Agreement”), pursuant to which
       Nanshan Aluminium Group (excluding the Group) shall provide on-demand technical support services as the Group
       may request from time to time. Such services include, among others, technical service on equipment construction,
       installation and testing, on-site technical repair and maintenance guidance services, through specialists and technical
       personnel to assist in the production of alumina products, research and development support for alumina production
       technology, product inspection and quality control, product improvement, skills training and assessment, scientific and
       technological personnel and technical personnel training. Its term is for a period commencing from the Listing Date
       and ending on 31 December 2027 (both dates inclusive).

       Historical transaction amount
       The aggregate historical transaction amount paid by the Group was approximately RMB32.0 million for FY2025
       (FY2024: RMB45.0 million).

       Annual caps
       The annual caps under the Technical Support Framework Agreement are RMB40.0 million (equivalent to US$5.6
       million), RMB45.0 million (equivalent to US$6.3 million) and RMB50.0 million (equivalent to US$7.0 million) for each of
       the years ending 31 December 2025, 2026 and 2027, respectively.

           The aggregate annual caps for both the Raw Material Master Purchase Agreement and the Bauxite Supply
           Framework Agreement are US$143.0 million, US$204.1 million and US$255.3 million for FY2025, FY2026 and
           FY2027 respectively, comprising annual caps of US$23.0 million, US$24.1 million, and US$15.3 million under
           the Raw Material Master Purchase Agreement, and annual caps of US$120.0 million, US$180.0 million, and
           US$240.0 million under the Bauxite Supply Framework Agreement, respectively.

5.    Alumina Sales Contract
      Principal terms
      On 13 November 2024, BAI entered into an alumina sales contract with Press Metal, subsequently novated by Press
      Metal to Press Metal Bintulu Sdn. Bhd. (“PMB”) on 14 January 2025 (the “Alumina Sales Contract”), which provides
      the mechanism for the operation of sale and purchase of sandy calcined metallurgical grade alumina. It is envisaged
      that from time to time as required, individual sale agreements will be entered into between BAI and PMB. Each sale
      agreement for the sale and purchase of alumina from the alumina production projects will set out, among others, the
      specifications, price, payment terms, shipment schedules, delivery terms, and other usual conditions (including those
      dealing with weights and assays, title and risk, insurance requirements and termination rights) provided that such
      terms and conditions must be on normal commercial terms. The Alumina Sales Contract shall govern the sale and
      purchase of alumina from 1 January 2025 onwards. Its term is for a period of 10 years commencing from 1 January
      2025, provided that the parties agree to renegotiate the terms if Press Metal ceases to be a shareholder of BAI
      (whether direct or indirect).

      Historical transaction amounts and volumes
      For FY2025, the Group recorded alumina sales revenue of approximately US$419.5 million and sales volume
      of approximately 957,800 tons (FY2024: sales revenue of approximately US$485.9 million and sales volume of
      approximately 1,040,300 tons).

      Annual caps
      The maximum aggregate annual transaction volume under the Alumina Sales Contract shall not exceed 1,050,000
      tons for the financial year ending 2025, and 1,575,000 tons per annum for each of the financial years from 2026 to
      2035.

For details of the above continuing connected transactions, please refer to the section headed “Connected Transactions” of
the Prospectus.

The INEDs have confirmed that the continuing connected transactions are conducted in accordance with Rule 14A.55 of the
Listing Rules. The INEDs have reviewed the continuing connected transactions and have confirmed that:

(a)   the continuing connected transactions have been entered into in the ordinary and usual course of business of the
      Group;

(b)   the continuing connected transactions have been entered into on normal commercial terms or terms better to the
      Group than terms available to or from independent third parties; and

(c)   the terms of the agreement governing each of the continuing connected transactions are fair and reasonable and in
      the interests of the shareholders of the Company as a whole.

SHARE OPTION SCHEME
On 10 March 2025, the Company conditionally approved and adopted the Share Option Scheme in accordance with the
provision of Chapter 17 of the Listing Rules.

Purpose of Share Options Scheme
The purpose of the Share Option Scheme is to incentivise and reward participants of the Share Option Scheme
(“Participants”) who have contributed or may contribute to our Group and to encourage Participants to work towards
enhancing the value of our Company and its Shares for the benefit of our Company and the Shareholders as a whole.

Eligible Participants
The Participants include (i) any director, employee or consultant of any member of our Group (including persons who are
granted options(s) under this Share Option Scheme as an inducement to enter into employment contracts with any member
of our Group) and, for the avoidance of doubt, excludes any former employee unless such person qualifies as a Participant
in some other capacity; and (ii) any director, employee or consultant of the holding companies, fellow subsidiaries or
associated companies of our Company, who our Board considers, in its sole discretion, have contributed or will contribute
to our Group.

The eligibility of any of the Participants for grant(s) of share options shall be determined by our Board from time to time on
the basis of our Board’s opinion as to the Participant’s contribution to the success of our Group’s operations.

Total Number of Shares Available for Issue and Percentage
The total number of Shares which may be issued in respect of all options to be granted under the Share Option Scheme
and any other scheme(s) of our Company must not in aggregate exceed 10% of the total number of Shares in issue at the
time dealings in the Shares first commence on the Stock Exchange (i.e. not exceeding 58,823,530 Shares) (the “General
Scheme Limit”).

Within the General Scheme Limit, the total number of Shares which may be issued in respect of all options to be granted
to the Service Providers under the Share Option Scheme and any other schemes shall not in aggregate exceed 1%
of the number of the Shares in issue at the time dealings in the Shares first commence on the Stock Exchange (the
“Service Provider Sublimit”). The General Scheme Limit and the Service Provider Sublimit may be refreshed by ordinary
resolution of the Shareholders in general meeting every three years from the date of the Shareholders’ approval for the last
refreshment (or the adoption date).

As at the date of this annual report, the total number of securities available for issue under the Scheme was 35,103,530
Shares, representing approximately 5.7% of the issued Shares (excluding treasury shares).

Maximum Entitlement of Each Eligible Participant
The total number of shares issued and to be issued upon exercise of Options granted to each participant (including both
exercised and outstanding options) under the Share Option Scheme of the Company, in any 12-month period up to the date
of grant shall not exceed 1% of the Shares in issue.

Period within which the Option may be exercised
An Option may be exercised in accordance with the terms of the Share Option Scheme at any time during a period as the
Board may determine which shall not exceed 10 years from the date of grant subject to the provisions of early termination
thereof.




                                                                       Annual Report 2025   Nanshan Aluminium International Holdings Limited   53
     Report of the Directors

     Vesting Period
     The Share Options to be granted under the Share Option Scheme shall be subject to a minimum vesting period of 12
     months during which unvested Share Options shall not become vested and exercisable. Any shorter vesting period in
     respect of Share Options granted to Participants must be approved by the Board and/or the Remuneration Committee (for
     Share Options granted to the Directors or senior managers) at the Directors’ discretion.

     Performance Targets
     Share Options granted under the Scheme may or may not be attached with performance targets. Among the Share Options
     granted on 24 July 2025: (i) the first tranche of 11.12% of the Share Options granted have no performance target attached,
     and the grant was based on the past performance and potential of the Grantees, their experiences in the Group’s business,
     and their contributions to the business development of the Group; and (ii) the remaining 88.88% of the Share Options
     granted are subject to performance targets determined with reference to, including but not limited to, length of continued
     employment, business or financial performance results, annual corporate targets or goals achieved, relevant transaction
     milestones, individual performance, appraisal on contribution to the Group, and other key performance indicators as
     determined by the Board. The Board or the Remuneration Committee (if authorised by the Board) may in its absolute
     discretion set performance targets to be achieved before the exercise of Share Options granted to the Grantees.

     Clawback Mechanism
     The Share Options granted will be subject to the clawback mechanism as set out in the terms of the Share Option Scheme.
     In particular, in the event of cessation of a Grantee’s service or employment with the Group or the related entities by
     reason of, among others, willful disobedience of a lawful and reasonable order, misconduct, fraud or dishonesty, or habitual
     negligence in performance of his/her duties, the Board may at its absolute discretion forfeit all the outstanding Share
     Options granted to the relevant Grantees but not yet vested and exercised without the approval or consent by the relevant
     Grantee.

     Amount Payable on Acceptance
     The amount payable by the grantee of a Share Option to the Company on acceptance of the offer for the grant of a Share
     Option is HKD1.00.

     Basis of Determining the Exercise Price
     The exercise price shall be a price solely determined by the Board and notified to a participant and shall be at least the
     higher of: (i) the closing price of the shares as stated in the Stock Exchange’s daily quotations sheet on the date on which
     an Option is offered to a Participant (the “Offer Date”), which must be a business day; (ii) the average closing prices of
     the shares as stated in the Stock Exchange’s daily quotations sheets for the five (5) business days immediately preceding
     the Offer Date; and (iii) the nominal value of a share on the date of grant of the Share Option, provided that in the event of
     fractional prices, the exercise price per share shall be rounded upwards to the nearest whole cent.

     Remaining Life of the Scheme
     As at 31 December 2025, the remaining life of the Share Option Scheme is approximately 9.3 years.

     REMUNERATION COMMITTEE REVIEW
     The Remuneration Committee has reviewed and approved the grant of Share Options to Directors and senior management
     during the Reporting Period. On 24 July 2025, the Company granted 800,000 Share Options to Mr. Hao Weisong (Chairman
     of the Board, executive Director, chief executive officer and general manager) and 580,000 Share Options to Ms. Wang
     Yanli (Non-executive Director). The Remuneration Committee considers such grants appropriate, with the determination
     taking into account, among other factors, the grantees’ positions, past performance, experience in and contributions to
     the Group’s business. The grant of Share Options aligns the interests of the Grantees with those of the Group, recognises
     their contributions, and is consistent with the purpose of the Share Option Scheme. Although the first 11.2% of Share
     Options granted to the Directors and/or senior managers were not attached with performance targets, taking into account
     (i) the value of the Share Options is linked to the future market price of the Shares, which, in turn, depends on the business
     performance of the Group, the relevance grantees will be incentivised to directly contribute to the business performance
     of the Group, so as to enhance the long-term value of the Shares; and (ii) the Options are subject to the vesting schedule
     as specified and may lapse if the Grantee ceases to be an eligible participant under the Share Option Scheme, the
     Remuneration Committee is of the view that the grant of Options of such portion without performance targets serves to
     recognize the past contributions by the Grantees, is market competitive and aligns with the purpose of the Share Option
     Scheme.

     The Company by resolution in a general meeting or the Board may at any time terminate the operation of the Share Option
     Scheme and in such event no further Option will be offered but Option granted prior to such termination shall continue to be
     valid and exercisable in accordance with provisions of the Share Option Scheme.

     For details of the Share Option Scheme, please refer to the principal terms summarised in the section headed “D. Other
     Information — 1. Share Option Scheme” in Appendix V of the Prospectus.


     PENSION SCHEMES
     The employees of the Group’s subsidiaries which operate in mainland China are required to participate in a defined
     contribution pension scheme centrally operated by the local municipal government. These subsidiaries are required to
     contribute a certain proportion of its payroll costs to the central pension scheme. The only obligation of the Group with
     respect to the central pension scheme is to make the required contributions. No forfeited contribution under the central
     pension scheme is available to reduce the contribution payable in future years. The contributions are charged to profit or
     loss as they become payable in accordance with the rules of the central pension scheme.

     The subsidiaries of the Group in Indonesia contribute to a statutory retirement scheme comprising both defined contribution
     and defined benefit components in compliance with Indonesian Labor Law. The Group contributes a certain fixed
     percentage of eligible employees’ basic salaries to a state-managed defined contribution scheme. Under this scheme,
     there are no forfeited contributions available for the Group to reduce future contribution obligations. The state-managed
     retirement plan is responsible for the entire pension obligations payable to all retired employees, and the Group has no
     further obligations for the actual pension payments or post-retirement benefits beyond the annual contributions.




56   Nanshan Aluminium International Holdings Limited   Annual Report 2025
                                                                                            Report of the Directors

The Group also provides post-employment defined benefits to their employees in conformity with the requirements
of Indonesian Labor Law. As at 31 December 2025, the Group’s total defined benefit obligations were approximately
US$395,000 (FY2024: approximately US$339,000). No plan assets were maintained and no funding was allocated to cover
these obligations. The Group believes its internal financial resources are sufficient to meet these obligations as they become
due, and there was no material surplus or deficiency related to the defined benefit scheme as at 31 December 2025.

Details regarding these defined benefit obligations are disclosed in Note 21 to the consolidated financial statements.

The Group also participates in a pension scheme under the rules and regulations of the Mandatory Provident Fund Schemes
Ordinance (Cap. 485 of the laws of Hong Kong) (“MPF Scheme”) for all employees in Hong Kong, which is a defined
contribution retirement scheme. The contributions to the MPF Scheme are based on minimum statutory contribution
requirement of 5% of eligible employees’ relevant aggregate income and are charged to profit or loss as they become
payable in accordance with the rules of the MPF Scheme. No forfeited contribution under the MPF Scheme is available to
reduce the contribution payable in future years. The assets of this pension scheme are held separately from those of the
Group in independently administered funds.

ABOUT THE REPORT
This Environmental, Social and Governance Report is the second ESG Report (hereinafter referred to as the “Report” or
the “ESG Report”) released by Nanshan Aluminium International Holdings Limited (hereinafter referred to as “Nanshan
Aluminium International”, “we/us” or the “Company”). The Report is designed to give an objective and true view of
the Company’s strategies, policies, measures and achievements in terms of sustainable development, and focuses on the
disclosure of the Company’s information in environmental, social and governance (hereinafter referred to as “ESG”) aspects.

Basis of Preparation
The Report has been prepared pursuant to the Environmental, Social and Governance Reporting Guide as set out in
Appendix C2 to the Main Board Listing Rules of The Stock Exchange of Hong Kong Limited (hereinafter referred to as the
“Hong Kong Stock Exchange”).

Reporting Cycle
From 1 January 2025 to 31 December 2025 (hereinafter referred to as the “Reporting Period” or the “Year”), but certain
information may relate to periods beyond the Reporting Period.

Reporting Boundary
The reporting scope of the Report covers Nanshan Aluminium International Holdings Limited (02610.HK) and its
subsidiaries, which is in line with the 2025 annual report of the Company.

Source of Information and Guarantee for Reliability
Unless otherwise stated, data contained herein are derived from the internal information, investigation and interview
records and relevant documents of the Company. The Board of the Company undertakes that the Report contains no false
information or misleading statement, and is responsible for its truthfulness, accuracy and completeness.

Confirmation and Approval
The Report has been approved by the Board on 26 March 2026 upon confirmation by the management.

Availability
The Report is incorporated in the 2025 annual report of the Company. Out of concern for environmental protection,
we recommend you to read the electronic version which is available at the website of the Hong Kong Stock Exchange
(www.hkexnews.hk) and the Company website (www.nanshanintl.com).

     1. ESG Governance

           Nanshan Aluminium International continues to optimize and improve its ESG governance structure, systematically
           promoting the Company’s transition towards sustainable development. The Board actively plays a strategic leadership
           role, strengthening internal cross-departmental collaboration and operational efficiency, laying a solid governance
           foundation for sustainable development. The Company has deeply integrated ESG requirements into its corporate
           strategy and future development plans, committing to building an efficient and scientifically structured internal
           governance system.


     1.1   ESG Governance Structure
           Nanshan Aluminium International has fully integrated ESG requirements into its corporate strategy and future
           development plans, committed to building an efficient and scientifically structured internal governance system.

           The Company has established an ESG governance structure with the Board of Directors at its core. Acting as the
           highest decision-making and oversight body for ESG matters, the Board is directly responsible for identifying and
           assessing ESG-related risks and opportunities, conducting materiality assessments, formulating ESG strategies and
           medium-to-long-term plans, and setting overall targets. It also regularly tracks implementation progress and target
           achievement to ensure the effective execution and continuous optimization of the ESG management system. Under
           the leadership of the Board, the Company utilizes a cross-departmental collaboration mechanism to systematically
           advance the execution, performance tracking, information disclosure, and continuous improvement of ESG initiatives.
           This ensures that ESG goals are embedded throughout the Company's operational processes, enhancing the
           professionalism, standardization and institutionalization of governance.

           Based on this clearly defined ESG management structure, we have formulated and continue to refine ESG-related
           systems and policies in light of both internal operational realities and external sustainability requirements providing
           an institutional basis and an operational framework for standardized effective advancement of each work. These
           policy documents cover core areas of ESG governance — including emissions and resource management, climate
           change response, employee rights and occupational health and safety, and supply chain sustainability — providing an
           institutional basis and an operational framework for the advancement of relevant work.

           Board Statement

                                                          decision-making deployment of major ESG matters, including the identification

     2. Responsibility Governance

           Nanshan Aluminium International attaches great importance to the continuous optimization of its governance
           system. By refining internal structures and mechanisms, the Company strives to enhance governance effectiveness.
           The Company constantly improve our decision-making, supervision, and risk control systems, clarify roles and
           responsibilities, and boost operational efficiency. These efforts drive the evolution of governance toward greater
           transparency and robustness, providing solid support for the Company's long-term development.

     2.1   Corporate Governance
           Nanshan Aluminium International has built an efficient and scientifically structured corporate governance system, and
           strictly complies with the relevant laws, regulations and guidelines such as the Investment Law, the Indonesian Limited
           Liability Company Law and the Corporate Governance Code of the Hong Kong Stock Exchange, effectively ensuring
           the rationality, transparency and compliance of the Company’s decision-making mechanism, and laying a solid
           governance foundation for sustainable development.

           Nanshan Aluminium International has established a systematic governance structure consisting of the general meeting
           of shareholders, the Board and three board committees. Through a clear hierarchical authorisation and collaboration
           mechanism, it transmits and implements governance responsibilities at all levels, thus providing a solid institutional
           guarantee for the scientificity, standardization and sustainability of the Company’s daily business activities.

           In 2025, the Company held 1 general meeting of shareholders, which reviewed and approved 6 resolutions; and 5
           meetings of the board of directors, with a 100% attendance rate of directors.

           We attach great importance to the rationality and scientificity of the board composition, continuously promote
           diversification and cultural integration, and constantly optimize the board structure. In the candidate selection process,
           we systematically evaluate multiple dimensions such as gender, age, educational background, industry experience and
           professional qualifications, and comprehensively assess their competence and team complementarity to continuously
           improve the overall decision-making level of the board and the efficiency of corporate governance.

2.2   Risk Management
      Nanshan Aluminium International focuses on building a full-chain management system covering the entire process
      of risk identification, assessment, response, and monitoring. Through regular risk awareness and capacity-building
      programs, the Company ensures the effective implementation of its risk prevention and control mechanisms, thereby
      providing a solid foundation for its sustained, stable operations and long-term development.

                                            Internal and External Risk Dimensions

2.3   Business Ethics
      Nanshan Aluminum International strictly adheres to the business ethics and anti-corruption laws and regulations
      of its operating locations and international standards, including but not limited to, among others, the Anti-Unfair
      Competition Law, the Transparency International’s Anti-Corruption Corporate Guidelines, the Republic of Indonesia’s
      Anti-Bribery Law and Anti-Corruption Law. Based on these, the Company has formulated the PT. Bintan Alumina
      Indonesia Anti-Corruption and Anti-Bribery System, which clearly defines codes of conduct, division of responsibilities,
      and accountability mechanisms. Through continuous training, supervision and review, and transparent reporting, the
      Company systematically builds an honest and compliant operating environment.

      We have established internal procedures such as the Financial Audit Management Policy and the Procurement
      Audit Management Policy, conducting regular integrity audits for personnel in key positions. These audits focus
      on nodes of power operation within critical business processes, emphasizing the verification of the authenticity of
      conflict-of-interest declarations, the compliance of commercial decisions and interactions, and the implementation
      status of relevant systems. The Audit Department handles audit results in accordance with regulations. For any
      suspected or confirmed violations of integrity standards identified during audits, the Company will conduct strict
      investigations and initiate formal inquiry procedures as required. Investigation results are reported through proper
      channels, and accountability measures are enforced to systematically prevent the occurrence of corruption, bribery,
      money laundering, unfair competition, and other non-compliant behaviors.

      During the Reporting Period, the Company was not aware of any incidents violating business ethics internally, and no
      employees were involved in any lawsuits related to embezzlement.

      The Company has established a systematic and incorruptible whistleblowing and protection mechanism, encouraging
      internal and external stakeholders to report (by name or anonymously) suspected corruption, bribery, abuse of power,
      improper benefit transfers, and other violations through multiple channels, including dedicated email, a whistleblowing
      hotline, and on-site mailboxes. The Company ensures that all reported information is independently investigated,
      promptly handled, and kept strictly confidential, and verified issues are seriously pursued according to regulations. To
      maintain the credibility of the whistleblowing mechanism, we strictly implement the Whistleblower Protection Policy,
      prohibiting any form of obstruction, interference, or retaliation. We protect whistleblower information comprehensively
      through technical and management measures. Additionally, recognising the importance of internal supervision, we
      provide corresponding rewards to whistleblowers based on the importance of the information provided, thereby
      continuously consolidating an open, transparent, and incorruptible compliant operating environment.

     2. Responsibility Governance

           We place high importance on internal integrity culture construction, continuously enhancing the compliance awareness
           and professional ethics of all employees through normalised, comprehensive integrity training and specialised
           education. For high-risk positions such as procurement and marketing, the Company conducts further specialised
           intensive training. The content closely focuses on job characteristics and business processes, deeply analysing
           potential risks like conflicts of interest, commercial bribery, and supply chain fraud, systematically enhancing relevant
           personnel’s risk identification and compliant performance capabilities. Through this multi-level, differentiated integrity
           education system, the Company actively fosters a culture of integrity, lawfulness, and self-discipline, integrating
           integrity into the daily conduct of every employee.

           During the Reporting Period and up to the date of this Report, the Company provided anti-corruption special training
           for all directors, with a 100% participation rate. Concurrently, the Company provided employees with an average of 1
           hour of anti-corruption training per person, totalling 173 hours and covering 173 employees.

     2.4   Information Security
           Nanshan Aluminium International strictly complies with laws and regulations in its operating locations, such as the
           Personal Data Protection Law, and has systematically built an internal management system centred on the PT.
           Bintan Alumina Indonesia Information Security and Privacy Protection Policy. We have established a comprehensive
           information security governance structure and management system to ensure all related work is conducted
           compliantly within the legal framework, continuously strengthening the protection of the Company’s and its related
           parties’ information assets.

           The Company has established a systematic internal information security management framework to operate
           responsibly and protect the rights of all parties. This system covers stringent account and access governance,
           technical de-identification and permission control for sensitive employee information, and defence-in-depth measures
           including endpoint protection, system hardening, and critical data backup. In terms of access rights management,
           we implement strict controls; all permissions require submission of a Network Information Department Business
           Application Form and approval through a process verifying business necessity, preventing redundant permissions
           for unrelated personnel. Simultaneously, we ensure the entire data transmission process is traceable. These
           institutionalised control measures, combined with regular awareness campaigns and audits, form the governance
           foundation for the Company to prevent internal risks and enhance operational resilience.

           During the Reporting Period, the Company experienced no data security or information leakage incidents.

           The Company places high importance on the continuous development of information security capabilities, fortifying
           its internal defences through systematic training and audit mechanisms. We regularly organise specialised safety
           awareness and skills training for all employees and key positions, and conduct practical attack-defence drills,
           effectively enhancing risk prevention and emergency response capabilities.

                                                                              2. Responsibility Governance


      Specialised Network Security Training

In 2025, to systematically enhance employees’ network security awareness and prevention capabilities, the
Company organised specialised network security training. We invited external security experts to conduct intensive
lectures for heads of various business units, focusing on network threat landscapes, common attack methods, and
daily protection points. Following the training, we required attending managers to convey the core content to all
their staff, ensuring safety awareness education effectively cascaded down. This initiative aims to build a security
culture of “management driving, all-employee participation”, fortifying the internal risk prevention and control line
through regular education and improving overall security protection levels.

     3.1   Product Quality
           Nanshan Aluminium International adheres to the quality management policy of “Independent Innovation, Scientific
           Management, and Continuous Improvement”, committed to providing reliable and stable high-quality products to
           global customers. We have established a product quality management system covering the entire process, from
           production to quality inspection, clearly defining the quality management responsibilities of management and all
           executing departments.

     3.1.1 Quality Management
           The Company conducts quality management in compliance with relevant national and local laws and regulations,
           combined with internal policies. During the Reporting Period, we issued and implemented the Alumina Product Quality
           Control Measures, aiming to integrate customer needs, production processes, management systems, and market
           feedback. This forms a closed-loop mechanism for continuously improving product quality, enhancing corporate
           competitiveness, driving enterprise growth, and ensuring product quality meets customer needs.


                     Quality Management System Certification

            During the Reporting Period, Nanshan Aluminium International obtained ISO 9001 Quality Management System
            certification and completed the 2025 annual review and company-wide training on the quality management
            system. We invited a third party institution to provide systematic training for our internal audit specialists, covering
            the organisational scope, quality management objectives and targets, process risk control, and continuous
            improvement. Through this annual review, all employees understood and participated in the ISO 9001 system
            internal audit and annual surveillance process, further deepening quality awareness.

                                                                                       3. Product Responsibility


       Nanshan Aluminium International Launches DCS (Distributed Control System)
       to Ensure Product Quality					

We adopted a “decentralized control, centralized management” structure for our DCS system, developing multiple
functions such as data acquisition, real-time monitoring, and automatic adjustment. This enables precise control of
the production process, reduces equipment failure rates, and ensures stable production operations. In addition, our
integrated data platform can record information including production data, laboratory test data, inspection records,
and equipment maintenance logs. This provides a solid basis for production process control and guarantees
consistent product quality.




During the Reporting Period, we formulated and successfully achieved the quality management objectives of “100%
product qualification rate” and “100% product completion rate”.

Product Recall Management
Nanshan Aluminium International strictly complies with applicable laws and regulations in its operating locations,
establishing product liability and recall management systems to standardise the process of identifying, assessing, and
managing potential quality risks. The Company clearly defines the triggering conditions, internal approval procedures,
and response measures for product recalls. Through quality traceability and risk monitoring mechanisms, we
strengthen the management of the entire product lifecycle.

In 2025, the Company further refined the product risk management and recall procedures, clarifying key steps such
as risk identification, internal assessment, customer communication, and corrective actions. We strengthened cross-
departmental coordination mechanisms and responsibility allocation, ensuring that, when necessary, recalls or risk
mitigation procedures can be initiated promptly and standardly, effectively reducing potential impacts on customers
and business operations.

During the Reporting Period, the Company had no product recalls due to quality issues, safety/health issues, or
customer complaints. The percentage of total products sold or shipped subject to recalls for safety and health reasons
was zero.

           Industry Communication

                     Nanshan Aluminium International Participates in China International Aluminium Week

             Nanshan Aluminium International upholds the concept of green intelligent manufacturing. The Company actively
             participated in industry communication events such as the China International Aluminum Week and conducted
             thematic seminars on key emission aspects of electrolytic aluminum production, specifically focusing on anode
             net consumption. As a critical factor influencing the carbon emission and production efficiency of electrolytic
             aluminum, anode net consumption is one of the core technical issues for the industry to achieve carbon reduction
             and quality improvement. This seminar aims to explore the optimal process pathway to ensure that future
             production activities maximize resource utilization efficiency while meeting environmental performance standards,
             thereby fulfilling the Company’s long-term commitment to sustainable development.

           trademarks, and technological achievements. Relevant departments legally and actively combat infringement; upon

3.3.1 Supplier Management
    Nanshan Aluminium International has formulated and implemented internal supplier management systems, such as the
    Nanshan Aluminium International Procurement Management Policy, which standardizes the full lifecycle management
    procedures and requirements for supplier admission, evaluation, cooperation, supervision, and elimination. Meanwhile,
    we require our suppliers to comply with laws and regulations related to the environment, procurement, and human
    rights in countries or regions where we operate.

    The Company has constructed a 4-tier supply chain management system, clearly defining responsibilities at each level.
    Through cross-departmental coordination mechanisms, we systematically carry out annual procurement management
    and supply chain operations, ensuring procurement activities are conducted standardly and efficiently.

                      4-tier Supplier Management Structure of Nanshan Aluminium International

    In addition, we are actively promoting the digital transformation of the supply chain and building a Supplier
    Digitalization System (NC System). This enables the online and centralized management of core processes such
    as procurement orders, supplier information, and contract execution, thereby enhancing the efficiency of supplier
    management and our risk control capabilities.

           Supplier Onboarding
           Nanshan Aluminium International has established and implemented a standardized supplier selection and admission
           process in accordance with policies such as Supplier Onboarding Management and New Suppliers Management
           Policy, while formulating clear screening criteria. For suppliers of different types, we establish differentiated
           qualification requirements, including relevant certifications such as ISO 9001 quality management system certification
           and ISO 14001 environmental management system certification. While meeting the basic entry requirements, the
           Company further leverages supplier full lifecycle assessment mechanism to conduct systematic evaluations. The
           scoring system covers key dimensions including product quality, occupational health and safety, business ethics, and
           environmental management, aiming to continuously enhance supply chain management capabilities and risk control.

           We classify suppliers into categories A, B and C based on their material categories and materiality, and implement
           different admission processes and regular management strategies for each category.

           Supplier Evaluation and Rectification
           The Company regularly conducts comprehensive evaluations of different types of suppliers, focusing on key indicators
           such as product quality, delivery punctuality, and service level. For suppliers deemed unqualified or in breach of
           contract/non-compliant, the Company promptly issues written corrective action requests, requiring them to take
           corrective measures and rectify within a specified timeframe. Simultaneously, the Company continuously follows up on
           the progress and effectiveness of corrective actions to ensure stable and reliable supply chain operations.

           Supplier Disqualification
           The Company has established and continue to implement a mechanism for eliminating existing suppliers. We regularly
           review and re-evaluate suppliers. For suppliers whose product quality consistently fails to meet standards, refuse
           to cooperate or fail to take effective corrective actions during problem rectification, and violate business ethics, the
           Company resolutely terminates cooperation in accordance with regulations and includes them in the supplier blacklist
           to maintain supply chain compliance and security.




78   Nanshan Aluminium International Holdings Limited       Environmental, Social and Governance Report 2025
                                                                                                 3. Product Responsibility


                                                    Supplier Disqualification Policy


             Low Assessment Score

     •      For suppliers with assessment scores that are unacceptably low, if alternative suppliers exist in the market
            capable of providing equivalent products, processes, or services while fully guaranteeing supply continuity, the
            Company shall terminate procurement from such suppliers and revoke their status as qualified suppliers.

             Hindered Cooperation

     •      During the course of cooperation, if a supplier experiences issues such as product quality, delivery delays,
            or after-sales service problems, and refuses to cooperate in making corrections or fails to take effective
            corrective measures, the company will initiate the elimination mechanism and terminate the cooperation.

             Violation of Business Ethics

     •      Suppliers found to have engaged in violations of business ethics during the cooperation process will be
            disqualified and, depending on the severity of the situation, may be placed on the supplier blacklist. Such
            violations include, but are not limited to collusion, bid rigging, bid rotation, using shell companies, engaging in
            fraud, falsifying qualifications, and other similar misconduct.



3.3.2 Sustainable Supply Chain
     Nanshan Aluminium International integrates ESG requirements into the supplier lifecycle management system,
     continuously improving supply chain ESG assessment standards and enhancing supply chain compliance and
     transparency. We regularly conduct audits and assessments of core raw material suppliers, focusing on Quality
     Management System (QMS), Occupational Health and Safety (EHS), and Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR)
     performance. We strengthen suppliers’ management capabilities in environmental management, occupational health,
     labor rights, and human rights protection. We also insist on implementing incorruptible procurement and supervision
     mechanisms to create a fair and transparent cooperation environment.

           Elimination of Conflict Minerals
           The Company aligns with ASI standards by incorporating key elements such as conflict minerals control, carbon
           footprint tracing, and raw material traceability into supplier entry clauses, and ensures the traceability of raw and
           auxiliary materials through a compliance review mechanism. In accordance with ASI guidelines, we collaborate
           exclusively with suppliers holding valid mining development permits (RKAB) and environmental licenses issued by
           the Indonesian government. We require all core suppliers to sign a Commitment Letter on Non-Use of Conflict
           Minerals and submit a Conflict Mineral Due Diligence Report, thereby ensuring that these suppliers have satisfied
           relevant review requirements imposed by the Indonesian government regarding environmental protection and social
           responsibility during the permitting process.


                     Nanshan Aluminium International Undertakes

            Not to support or use any metals derived from armed conflict, illegal mining, or poor working environment, namely
            “conflict minerals”. Suppliers shall investigate the gold (Au), tantalum (Ta), stannum (Sn), tungsten (W), and
            aluminium (Al) in their products and identify the source of these metals in accordance with OECD Due Diligence
            Guidance for Responsible Supply Chains of Minerals from Conflict-Affected and High-Risk Areas2.




     2       https://mneguidelines.oecd.org/duediligence/

                                                                                        3. Product Responsibility

During the Reporting Period, all of the Company’s mineral raw materials were procured within Indonesia, without
involvement in the use of conflict minerals.

Supply Chain Resilience
Nanshan Aluminium International continuously optimises procurement strategies for key materials like bauxite and
coal, establishing stable cooperative relationships with multiple high-quality local suppliers in Indonesia. We have
signed long-term supply agreements with large bauxite and coal suppliers, continuously strengthening the supply
assurance capability for key raw materials and comprehensively enhancing supply chain resilience and operational
safety.

Supply Chain Integrity
Nanshan Aluminium International continuously implements the Nanshan Aluminium International Anti-Corruption
Policy, continuously deepening the supervision and management of employee and partner behaviour. We require all
cooperating suppliers to sign Sunshine Commitment Letter and Integrity Commitment Letter, continuously fulfilling
social responsibilities and jointly creating a fair, just, open, and transparent sunshine procurement environment.


       Excerpt from the Sunshine Commitment Letter

•     We undertake and guarantee that we will strictly abide by all relevant laws and regulations when we carry out
      business activities with Nanshan Aluminium International. We will adhere to the principles of openness, justice,
      fairness, and good faith, and we will not seek illegitimate benefits or transaction opportunities, nor will we
      engage in any improper transaction practices

•     We promise and guarantee that all relevant licenses, qualifications, approvals, permits, certificates, and other
      documents required for the relevant business are held in compliance with laws and regulations, and that the
      provided goods meet the technical requirements specified by the Company

•     We undertake and guarantee that we will not implement any price or sales policy discrimination against
      Nanshan Aluminium International, and our transaction prices of goods and sales policies will not be higher or
      more rigorous than those offered by any third party in the market

•     We promise to maintain the confidentiality of any business information and trade secrets of Nanshan
      Aluminium International that we and our representatives acquire during the bidding process


The audit department of the Company supervises the entire procurement process through ex-ante, during, and ex-
post stages, and regularly conducts special audits and compliance inspections to ensure that procurement activities
are carried out in accordance with laws and regulations, preventing non-compliant operations and violations of
business ethics.

In addition, the Company has established reporting channels such as a hotline and an email address for reporting
disciplinary violations, which are open to employees and other stakeholders to ensure an effective and unimpeded
reporting mechanism.

     3. Product Responsibility

     3.3.3 Supplier Empowerment and Risk Management
           Nanshan Aluminium International values establishing long-term, stable cooperative relationships with suppliers.
           Through continuous empowerment, communication, and collaborative improvement mechanisms, we drive suppliers’
           continuous improvement in quality management, environmental management, occupational health and safety, and
           social responsibility.

           Supplier Empowerment
           The Company focuses on continuously enhancing supplier capabilities across three dimensions — environment,
           responsibility compliance, and management — following international standards like ASI criteria and the Responsible
           Business Alliance (RBA) Code of Conduct, as well as local Indonesian regulations. We insist on integrating green
           and low-carbon concepts into supply chain management requirements, setting phased goals for key issues such
           as ISO 14001 certification coverage, green packaging replacement, and carbon footprint traceability. Through
           measures including order management, procurement ratings, and cooperation level adjustments, we guide suppliers
           to continuously improve environmental performance. The Company also encourages suppliers to prioritise clean
           energy, adopt integrated fixed devices to replace disposable packaging, eliminate container packaging, and
           implement energy-saving and emission-reduction measures, jointly promoting supply chain decarbonisation and green
           transformation.

           By continuously promoting supplier rectification, improvement, and target guidance mechanisms, we continuously
           strengthen supply chain resilience and sustainable development capabilities, facilitating coordinated high-quality and
           responsible development between the Company and its suppliers. As of the end of the reporting period, the coverage
           rate of ISO 14001 among the Company’s core suppliers has reached 80%.

           Supplier Risk Management
           To maintain good communication with our suppliers, we communicate with our long-term suppliers via phone or email
           every month. Through communication, we discuss in depth the Company’s requirements on product quality and
           communicate in detail about the near-term procurement and delivery plans, ensuring a smooth coordination between
           supply and demand, and stable and controllable delivery.

           For our mineral raw material suppliers, we conduct on-site inspections every 2–3 months to gain a practical
           understanding of mining production and logistics operations, while also verifying supplier performance and identifying
           any violations of cooperation agreements. Through these regular on-site visits, the Company can promptly identify and
           assess potential quality, environmental and human rights risks, take preventive and corrective measures in advance,
           and continuously enhance its supply chain risk management capabilities and operational resilience.




82   Nanshan Aluminium International Holdings Limited   Environmental, Social and Governance Report 2025
                                                                                                   4. Harmonious Ecology

      Nanshan Aluminium International adheres to the concept of green and low-carbon development, actively addressing
      challenges posed by climate change. We integrate environmental protection and resource conservation into daily
      operations management. The Company continuously improves its environmental management system, strengthens
      pollution prevention and control measures, ensures stable and compliant discharge of various pollutants, and
      continuously enhances energy and resource utilisation efficiency. We promote the implementation of cleaner
      production and energy-saving and consumption-reduction initiatives, fostering the continuous improvement of the
      Company’s sustainable development capabilities.

4.1   Climate Change Response
      Climate change has become a major global challenge, profoundly impacting the ecological environment, economic
      development, and social well-being. In recent years, international attention to climate issues has continuously
      increased, and related policies and regulatory requirements have become increasingly stringent. As a responsible
      enterprise, we place high importance on the risks and opportunities brought by climate change, actively fulfilling
      our social responsibility to mitigate and adapt to climate change, continuously promoting green and low-carbon
      development, and contributing to addressing the global climate challenge.

4.1.1 Governance
      The Company continues to refine climate change management system and is progressively establishing a climate
      governance structure that covers strategy formulation, risk identification, target management, and performance
      monitoring. Under the overall leadership of the Board, the Company oversees and reviews the progress in
      implementing climate-related strategies, policies, and objectives. The Board coordinates the identification,
      assessment, and implementation of response measures Company’s for climate risks and opportunities, ensuring that
      climate risk management is integrated into the corporate governance and operational decision-making systems to
      continuously enhance climate resilience and sustainable development capabilities.

4.1.2 Strategies
      Nanshan Aluminium International places high importance on the potential impact of climate-related risks and
      opportunities on strategy and finance. We employ scenario analysis to assess the potential impact of climate-related
      risks and opportunities on the Company’s financial performance under different climate scenarios, integrating short-,
      medium-, and long-term business planning, policy trends, and macro-environmental changes. Regarding physical
      risks, the Company selected the Shared Socioeconomic Pathways SSP2–4.5 and SSP5–8.5 scenarios from the
      Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC) for analysis; regarding transition risks, we conducted assessments
      referencing the Net Zero Emissions by 2050 Scenario (NZE) and the Announced Pledges Scenario (APS) of the
      International Energy Agency (IEA).




                                                Environmental, Social and Governance Report 2025   Nanshan Aluminium International Holdings Limited   83
     4. Harmonious Ecology

           Scenario   Climate
           Assumption Scenario                            Scenario Description


           Physical            SSP2-4.5                   SSP2-4.5 is a medium emissions scenario, assuming some global
           Risks                                          implementation of mitigation policies and climate actions, but with limited overall
                                                          ambition. Under this scenario, global greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions are
                                                          projected to peak around mid-century and then gradually decline, with global
                                                          mean temperatures estimated to rise by approximately 2.4°C to 3.0°C by 2100
                                                          compared to pre-industrial levels.

                               SSP5-8.5                   SSP5-8.5 is a high emissions scenario, assuming global development relies
                                                          heavily on fossil fuels with weak climate policies. Under this scenario, GHG
                                                          emissions continue to grow throughout the century, leading to significantly
                                                          higher temperature increases than in other scenarios.


           Transition          NZE                        The NZE scenario is a technologically feasible and cost-effective pathway
           Risks                                          projected by the IEA to achieve net-zero CO2 emissions from the global energy
                                                          and industrial systems by around 2050, limiting global warming to 1.5°C
                                                          through energy restructuring, energy efficiency improvements, and technological
                                                          innovation.

                               APS                        The Announced Pledges Scenario (APS) simulates future pathways for energy
                                                          systems and GHG emissions based on climate targets and policy commitments
                                                          announced by governments. This scenario considers the potential impacts of
                                                          policy implementation on energy demand structure, energy prices, and emission
                                                          levels.


           Furthermore, Nanshan Aluminium International, referencing the IFRS S2 Climate-related Disclosures, systematically
           identifies, assesses, and manages climate-related risks and opportunities, formulating and implementing
           corresponding response strategies. We conduct systematic assessments of the likelihood and potential impacts of
           climate-related risks and opportunities across three time horizons: short-term (within 1 year), medium-term (2–3 years),
           and long-term (over 3 years), integrating the analysis with the Company’s business layout and development plans.

           Building on this, we are progressively integrating climate-related management requirements into business decision-
           making and production operations. By continuously improving our risk identification, assessment, and response
           mechanisms, we aim to enhance our climate risk management capabilities and operational resilience, supporting the
           Company’s long-term stable development.

                                                                                              4. Harmonious Ecology

Climate-related risks and transition risks are increasingly becoming key factors influencing corporate operations and
strategic decisions. Based on this year’s climate scenario analysis, we conclude that the physical risks identified above
do not pose a significant financial impact on Nanshan Aluminium International. Under both the SSP2-4.5 and SSP5-
8.5 scenarios, our analysis indicates that physical risks are overall low to medium risk and currently do not constitute
a significant financial impact. Transition risks are predominantly medium risk and require ongoing attention and
integration into medium-to-long-term strategic management.

Through systematic assessment and analysis, the Company further identifies and evaluates the potential impacts of
climate-related risks and opportunities on business activities and production operations. We conduct GHG emissions
inventories to gain a comprehensive understanding of our emission management status and continuously advance
carbon reduction initiatives in line with our low-carbon development plan. This enhances our ability to address climate
risks and seize opportunities presented by green transformation. Centred on our climate change response objectives,
Nanshan Aluminium International has formulated three core strategic pathways:


       Energy Conservation and Consumption Reduction Initiatives

•     Optimize production processes, upgrade equipment, and retrofit facilities to enhance energy efficiency;
      integrate energy-saving and emission-reduction measures into manufacturing and operational management

       Energy Reuse

•     Maximize the reduction of energy waste and improve energy utilization efficiency through waste heat recovery
      and cascade utilization

       Renewable Energy Substitution

•     Research feasible solutions for renewable energy utilization to replace fossil fuels with clean energy and
      promote low-carbon transformation


Carbon Inventory and Tracking
During the Reporting Period, the Nanshan Aluminium International GHG task force completed the preparation of
the GHG-related work framework. They compiled several reports, including the Final Report on Carbon Economic
Value Consulting and Guidance for the Electric Power Production Sub-sector of PT. Bintan Alumina Indonesia, the
Preliminary Feasibility Study on Mitigation Action Potential in the Energy Sector of PT. Bintan Alumina Indonesia,
the PT BAE-PU Power Plant Emission Cap and RME Mitigation Plan Report, the Greenhouse Gas Inventory and

management documents, including the power plant emission monitoring plan. Detailed greenhouse gas emission data
are provided in the “Metrics and Targets” chapter of this report.

    Clean Energy Utilisation
    The Company actively supports the green development plan of the industrial park in the Galang Batang Special
    Economic Zone where it is located. Taking into account the peak-shaving capacity of the power generating units
    and the park’s developable photovoltaic (PV) resources, the Company explores the feasibility of utilising renewable
    energy. Currently, a PV project layout covering approximately 30 hectares of usable area is being advanced within the
    industrial park. The Company will continue to follow up on the project progress, and in accordance with the park’s
    overall development plan, gradually increase the proportion of renewable energy usage.

4.1.3 Risk Management
    Nanshan Aluminium International continuously improves its climate-related risk management system, integrating
    climate-related risks into the Company’s sustainable development and overall risk management framework. This forms
    a management process encompassing risk identification, assessment, response, and continuous monitoring. We have
    established a routine reporting mechanism to periodically report to the Board on climate change risk management
    performance and progress against related targets. This ensures the effective implementation of climate risk
    management measures and continuously enhances the Company’s operational resilience and sustainable development
    capabilities.

     4.1.4 Metrics and Targets
           Nanshan Aluminium International deeply recognises that low-carbon transition has become a crucial pathway for
           achieving high-quality and sustainable development in the aluminium manufacturing industry. Actively responding to
           the Group’s overall “Dual Carbon” strategic goals, and based on a systematic analysis of our own energy consumption
           and GHG emissions status, combined with internal and external professional opinions, we have formulated energy
           use efficiency improvement targets and related management measures. The Company continuously tracks progress
           against targets and conducts performance assessments, regularly reviewing target achievement to drive the
           continuous optimisation and implementation of energy-saving, consumption-reduction, and low-carbon operational
           measures.

           Group Target: Achieve carbon peak by 2030 and carbon neutrality by 2050.

           Energy Use Efficiency Target: By 2030, comprehensive energy consumption intensity of the product shall
           be maintained at no more than 0.6 tonnes of standard coal equivalent (tce) per tonne of product.

           During the Reporting Period, Nanshan Aluminium International’s indicators for GHG emissions 6 and energy

      supervision and guidance, and the Company’s Safety and Environmental Protection Department responsible for
      promoting the implementation of environmental protection responsibilities. This continuously enhances environmental

Enhancing Environmental Protection Awareness
Nanshan Aluminium International values the development of employees’ environmental management capabilities,
conducting systematic training and regular environmental drills to strengthen company-wide environmental awareness.
We organise environment-related training and emergency drills according to the annual plan, continuously enhancing
employees’ abilities and awareness in environmental management, standardised operations, and occupational health
protection. Training covers environmental laws and regulations, pollution prevention measures, risk identification, and
emergency response procedures.

During the Reporting Period, the Company conducted 58 environment-related training sessions, with a cumulative
participation of 1,188 attendees. Through systematic training and continuous supervision, the Company further
strengthened its environmental risk prevention and control capabilities, with no environmental pollution incidents
occurring during the year.

     4.2.2 Emissions and Waste
           Nanshan Aluminium International consistently adheres to the philosophy of “promoting cleaner production and
           achieving sustainable development,” systematically advancing pollution prevention and control across all production
           processes. The Company strictly follows laws, regulations, and regulatory requirements for pollutant emissions in
           its operating locations, implementing full-process management for exhaust gas, wastewater, and solid waste. It has
           clearly defined the core environmental management target of 100% compliant emission for all types of pollutants.
           Furthermore, we proactively set more stringent internal control standards, optimise production processes, strengthen
           source control, and promote resource recycling. This continuously reduces pollutant emission intensity, minimises
           environmental impact, and steadily enhances our green manufacturing capabilities.

           Management of Exhaust Gas
           Nanshan Aluminium International strictly implements exhaust gas emission regulations in its operating locations, such
           as the Indonesian Thermal Power Plant Emission Quality Standards, ensuring all emission indicators consistently meet
           regulatory requirements and clearly setting the management target of 100% compliant emissions of exhaust gas..

           The Company has installed online monitoring systems in all production plants, implementing uninterrupted monitoring
           and data evaluation for major air pollutants. Comparative monitoring and validity audits are conducted to ensure the
           accuracy, completeness, and traceability of monitoring data. In terms of control measures, the factory is equipped
           with treatment facilities such as electrostatic precipitators and baghouse dust collectors to effectively control dust and
           particulate matter concentrations. Meanwhile, desulfurisation towers are used for exhaust gas treatment to reduce
           sulphur dioxide emissions, minimising the impact of production operations on the surrounding environment.

                                                         Exhaust Gas Emission Reduction Measures




                                    Reduce the soot and dust content in exhaust gas through various types of dust collectors such as
                                    high-voltage electrostatic precipitators for power plant boilers, high-voltage electrostatic
                                    precipitators for alumina calciners, and baghouse dust collectors for coal gasifiers




                                    Utilisation of the power plant's seawater desulfurisation system and the coal gasification desulfurisation
                                    system to reduce the sulphide content in emissions




                                    Effectively control dust in the coal yard and reduce air particulate matter pollution through
                                    windbreak and dust suppression walls combined with water spraying
